In my causal SMEM kernel, the `__syncthreads()` after the score computation might be a problem. Let me trace through more carefully: ```cpp for (int tile_j = 0; tile_j < seq_len; tile_j += BN) { // Load K tile __syncthreads(); // Compute scores if (valid) { for (int j = 0; j < num_valid; ++j) { int global_j = tile_j + j; float score; if (global_j > q_pos) { score = -1e20f; } else { // compute dot product score = partial * scale; } if (lane == 0) { smem_s[warp_id * BN + j] = score; } } if (lane == 0) { for (int j = num_valid; j < BN; ++j) { smem_s[warp_id * BN + j] = -1e20f; } } __syncwarp(); // Tile max float local_max = -1e20f; for (int idx = lane; idx < BN; idx += WARP_SIZE) { int global_j = tile_j + idx; if (idx < num_valid && global_j <= q_pos) { float s = smem_s[warp_id * BN + idx]; local_max = fmaxf(local_max, s); } } for (int offset = WARP_SIZE / 2; offset > 0; offset /= 2) { local_max = fmaxf(local_max, __shfl_down_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, local_max, offset)); } float tile_max = __shfl_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, local_max, 0); if (q_pos >= tile_j) { float m_new = fmaxf(m, tile_max); float scale_factor = expf(m - m_new); for (int d = 0; d < ELEM_PER_THREAD; ++d) acc[d] *= scale_factor; l *= scale_factor; m = m_new; } } __syncthreads(); if (valid && q_pos >= tile_j) { // Load V tile __syncthreads(); // Accumulate __syncthreads(); } __syncthreads(); } ``` Wait, in the V loading and accumulation block, the `__syncthreads()` is inside the `if (valid && q_pos >= tile_j)` block. But `__syncthreads()` must be called by ALL threads in the block! If some threads skip it because q_pos < tile_j, that's undefined behavior! YES! This is the bug! In my causal SMEM kernel, I have: ```cpp if (valid && q_pos >= tile_j) { // Cooperative load of V tile into smem (reuse buffer) for (int idx = threadIdx.x; idx < BN * HEAD_SIZE; idx += blockDim.x) { ... } __syncthreads(); ... __syncthreads(); } __syncthreads(); ``` Actually, looking at the code more carefully, the `__syncthreads()` is inside the `if` block. But I also have another `__syncthreads()` at the end of the tile loop, outside the if block. Let me check... Looking at the actual code: ```cpp if (valid && q_pos >= tile_j) { // Load V tile for (...) {...} __syncthreads(); // Accumulate ... __syncthreads(); } __syncthreads(); ``` Wait, the inner `__syncthreads()` calls are inside the if block, and the outer `__syncthreads()` at the end of the tile loop is also there. So if `q_pos < tile_j`, threads skip the inner `__syncthreads()` calls and only hit the outer one. BUT the outer `__syncthreads()` is reached by all threads in the block (assuming valid is true for all threads in all warps, which it is if block_i + warp_id < seq_len for all warps in the block). Wait, but what about the first `__syncthreads()` after score computation? ```cpp } __syncthreads(); ``` This is outside the `if (valid)` block. But is it? Let me look at the actual code: ```cpp // Compute scores Q @ K^T for this tile with causal mask if (valid) { ... if (q_pos >= tile_j) { float m_new = fmaxf(m, tile_max); ... m = m_new; } } __syncthreads(); ``` Yes, the `__syncthreads()` is after the `if (valid)` block. So all threads in the block reach it. Good. But then: ```cpp if (valid && q_pos >= tile_j) { // Cooperative load of V tile into smem (reuse buffer) for (int idx = threadIdx.x; idx < BN * HEAD_SIZE; idx += blockDim.x) { ... } __syncthreads(); // Accumulate weighted V (only for unmasked positions) ... __syncthreads(); } __syncthreads(); ``` HERE'S THE BUG! Inside the `if (valid && q_pos >= tile_j)` block, there are `__syncthreads()` calls. If a warp has `q_pos < tile_j`, its threads do NOT enter this block and do NOT call the inner `__syncthreads()` calls. But they DO reach the final `__syncthreads()` outside the if block. The problem: the warps that DO enter the if block call `__syncthreads()` inside. The warps that DON'T enter skip those `__syncthreads()` calls. Then ALL warps reach the final `__syncthreads()`. Is this OK? Actually, it might be! Because `__syncthreads()` only requires that all threads in the block eventually reach a `__syncthreads()`. It doesn't matter if some threads reach a different `__syncthreads()` first, as long as they all ultimately reach the SAME barrier point. Wait, no. CUDA requires that all threads in the block execute `__syncthreads()` in a consistent manner. If some threads call it at one point and others at a different point, it's fine as long as they're conceptually at the same barrier. But within the SAME `__syncthreads()` call, all threads must participate. In my code: - Threads in warps with q_pos >= tile_j execute: `__syncthreads()` (inside), then `__syncthreads()` (inside), then `__syncthreads()` (outside) - Threads in warps with q_pos < tile_j execute: `__syncthreads()` (outside) For the OUTSIDE `__syncthreads()` at the end, all threads reach it. So this is valid. For the INSIDE `__syncthreads()` calls, only some threads reach them. CUDA says: "If any thread in a thread block issues a __syncthreads(), then all threads in the thread block must also issue a __syncthreads() or the behavior is undefined." OH! So having `__syncthreads()` inside a divergent `if` block is UB in CUDA! This means threads in some warps call `__syncthreads()` while threads in other warps don't. This can cause deadlocks or incorrect behavior! THIS IS THE BUG! In my causal SMEM kernel, the `__syncthreads()` inside the `if (valid && q_pos >= tile_j)` block causes UB! For example, if block_i = 0 and tile_j = 64: - Warp 0: q_pos = 0 < 64, does NOT enter the if block, does NOT call inner __syncthreads() - Warp 7: q_pos = 7 < 64, does NOT enter the if block - All warps reach the outer __syncthreads() at the end. OK. But if tile_j = 0: - Warp 0: q_pos = 0 >= 0, ENTERS the if block, calls inner __syncthreads() - Warp 7: q_pos = 7 >= 0, ENTERS the if block, calls inner __syncthreads() - ALL warps call the inner __syncthreads(). OK. If tile_j = 4: - Warp 0: q_pos = 0 < 4, does NOT enter - Warp 1: q_pos = 1 < 4, does NOT enter - Warp 2: q_pos = 2 < 4, does NOT enter - Warp 3: q_pos = 3 < 4, does NOT enter - Warp 4: q_pos = 4 >= 4, ENTERS, calls inner __syncthreads() - ... - Warp 7: q_pos = 7 >= 4, ENTERS, calls inner __syncthreads() Here, warps 0-3 skip the inner __syncthreads(), while warps 4-7 call it. This is undefined behavior! Oh my god, this might explain the correctness failure! But wait - does this bug also exist in my non-causal SMEM kernel? Let me check: ```cpp // Accumulate weighted V if (valid) { float local_sum = 0.0f; ... l += tile_sum; } __syncthreads(); ``` In the non-causal kernel, the `__syncthreads()` is OUTSIDE the `if (valid)` block. Inside the if block, there's just computation, no `__syncthreads()`. So the non-causal SMEM kernel doesn't have this issue. But the non-causal SMEM kernel ALSO fails with a similar error! So while this is a bug in the causal kernel, it's not the sole explanation for the correctness failure. Wait, let me check the non-causal SMEM kernel for `__syncthreads()` inside if blocks: Looking at the code: ```cpp // Load V tile into smem (reuse buffer) for (int idx = threadIdx.x; idx < BN * HEAD_SIZE; idx += blockDim.x) { ... } __syncthreads(); // Accumulate weighted V if (valid) { ... } __syncthreads(); ``` The `__syncthreads()` is OUTSIDE the `if (valid)` block. All threads call it. Good. And the V loading is not inside any if block. All threads participate. Good. So the non-causal SMEM kernel doesn't have the divergence bug. But it still fails! So there's still another issue. Actually, wait. Let me think about this differently. What if the reference model DOES use causal attention, and the non-causal kernel fails because it's computing full attention? And what if the causal kernel fails because of the `__syncthreads()` divergence bug? If I fix the causal kernel's `__syncthreads()` bug, it might pass! Let me write a fixed causal kernel where `__syncthreads()` is NEVER inside a divergent if block. Actually, I already submitted compile and correctness for the non-causal no-shared-memory kernel. It had the smallest error (0.059). But if the reference uses causal attention, a fixed causal kernel would be better. Let me quickly write a fixed causal no-shared-memory kernel and submit it. Actually, the no-shared-memory kernel doesn't use `__syncthreads()` at all! It's causal-safe because it's just one warp per block. Let me create a causal version of that. ```cpp // Each block = one query position int q_pos = blockIdx.x; // One warp per block int lane = threadIdx.x; // Load Q // For j = 0 to q_pos: <- causal: only process j <= q_pos // load K[j], compute score // if j > q_pos: score = -inf (skip) // online softmax // load V[j] // accumulate // For j = q_pos+1 to seq_len-1: skip ``` Wait, in a no-shared-memory kernel with one warp per block, I can just loop from j=0 to seq_len-1 and skip j > q_pos: ```cpp for (int j = 0; j < seq_len; ++j) { // load K[j] // compute score // if (j > q_pos) score = -1e20f; // online softmax float m_new = fmaxf(m, score); // ... m = m_new; // load V[j] // if (j <= q_pos) accumulate V[j] with p = exp(score - m) } ``` Wait, but if j > q_pos and score = -1e20f, then m_new will not change (since m starts at -1e20f and only increases when valid scores are encountered). But after we've seen some valid scores, m is a finite value. Then for j > q_pos: - score = -1e20f - m_new = fmaxf(m, -1e20f) = m (unchanged) - factor = expf(m - m_new) = expf(0) = 1 - l = l * 1 + expf(-1e20f - m) = l + 0 = l (unchanged) - acc *= 1 (unchanged) And then for V accumulation, j > q_pos, so we skip. This is correct! The online softmax handles the -inf scores automatically without changing the state. So I can write a causal no-shared-memory kernel just by adding an if statement around the V accumulation and setting score = -inf for masked positions. Wait, but I can also just early-exit the loop and avoid loading K/V for j > q_pos: ```cpp for (int j = 0; j <= q_pos; ++j) { // load K[j], compute score, online softmax, load V[j], accumulate } ``` This is simpler! For each query position q_pos, we only look at keys 0..q_pos. But then we'd still need to process j = 0 to q_pos. The number of iterations varies per block. Blocks with q_pos=0 have 1 iteration, blocks with q_pos=1023 have 1024 iterations. In terms of performance for a warp, the total work is the same as having all warps in a block cooperate. With independent blocks, each warp does its own loop. Since we're running with grid = [seq_len, num_heads, batch] = [1024, 32, 4] blocks, with each block having 1 warp (32 threads), we have 1024*32*4 = 131072 warps running in parallel. For causal attention, the total FLOPs is reduced by half compared to full attention (on average). This should be faster than full attention. Let me write this kernel. No shared memory, one warp per query position (one block per query position), causal. ```cpp template <int HEAD_SIZE> __global__ void gqa_causal_nosmem_kernel(...) { int q_pos = blockIdx.x; int head = blockIdx.y; int batch = blockIdx.z; int lane = threadIdx.x; int heads_per_kv = num_heads / num_kv_heads; int kv_head = head / heads_per_kv; // Load Q float q_reg[ELEM_PER_THREAD]; // ... float acc[ELEM_PER_THREAD] = {0}; float m = -1e20f; float l = 0.0f; // Causal: only attend to keys 0..q_pos for (int j = 0; j <= q_pos; ++j) { // Load K[j] // Compute score // Online softmax // Load V[j] // Accumulate } // Normalize and write } ``` This is clean and simple. Let me write this and submit it. But wait, for causal attention, q_pos can range from 0 to seq_len-1. When q_pos=0, the loop runs once. When q_pos=1023, it runs 1024 times. But because each block is independent, blocks with different q_pos take different amounts of time. This imbalance might hurt occupancy, but correctness is the priority. Actually for this exact reason, a better tiled approach might be to tile in the j dimension and have all query positions in a block process the same tile. But for simplicity, let me submit the per-query-position version. Also, the H100 has insane memory bandwidth, and for causal attention, each query only reads a fraction of the KV cache. This should be efficient enough. Let me write a clean kernel: ```cpp #include <torch/extension.h> #include <cuda_runtime.h> #include <cuda_fp16.h> #include <c10/cuda/CUDAStream.h> #define WARP_SIZE 32 template <int HEAD_SIZE> __global__ void gqa_causal_nosmem_kernel( const at::Half* __restrict__ q, const at::Half* __restrict__ k, const at::Half* __restrict__ v, at::Half* __restrict__ out, int num_heads, int num_kv_heads, int seq_len, float scale) { constexpr int ELEM_PER_THREAD = HEAD_SIZE / WARP_SIZE; constexpr int N_HALF2_PER_THREAD = ELEM_PER_THREAD / 2; int q_pos = blockIdx.x; int head = blockIdx.y; int batch = blockIdx.z; int lane = threadIdx.x; if (q_pos >= seq_len) return; int heads_per_kv = num_heads / num_kv_heads; int kv_head = head / heads_per_kv; // Load Q float q_reg[ELEM_PER_THREAD]; { int64_t q_off = ((int64_t)batch * num_heads + head) * seq_len + q_pos; const __half2* q_ptr2 = reinterpret_cast<const __half2*>(q + q_off * HEAD_SIZE); #pragma unroll for (int h2 = 0; h2 < N_HALF2_PER_THREAD; ++h2) { int idx = lane * N_HALF2_PER_THREAD + h2; __half2 val = q_ptr2[idx]; q_reg[h2 * 2 + 0] = __half2float(val.x); q_reg[h2 * 2 + 1] = __half2float(val.y); } } float acc[ELEM_PER_THREAD]; #pragma unroll for (int d = 0; d < ELEM_PER_THREAD; ++d) acc[d] = 0.0f; float m = -1e20f; float l = 0.0f; // Causal attention: query at position q_pos attends to keys 0..q_pos for (int j = 0; j <= q_pos; ++j) { // Load K[j] float k_reg[ELEM_PER_THREAD]; { int64_t k_off = ((int64_t)batch * num_kv_heads + kv_head) * seq_len + j; const __half2* k_ptr2 = reinterpret_cast<const __half2*>(k + k_off * HEAD_SIZE); #pragma unroll for (int h2 = 0; h2 < N_HALF2_PER_THREAD; ++h2) { int idx = lane * N_HALF2_PER_THREAD + h2; __half2 val = k_ptr2[idx]; k_reg[h2 * 2 + 0] = __half2float(val.x); k_reg[h2 * 2 + 1] = __half2float(val.y); } } // Dot product float partial = 0.0f; #pragma unroll for (int d = 0; d < ELEM_PER_THREAD; ++d) { partial += q_reg[d] * k_reg[d]; } #pragma unroll for (int offset = WARP_SIZE / 2; offset > 0; offset /= 2) { partial += __shfl_down_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, partial, offset); } float score = __shfl_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, partial, 0) * scale; // Online softmax float m_new = fmaxf(m, score); float factor = expf(m - m_new); if (lane == 0) { l = l * factor + expf(score - m_new); } #pragma unroll for (int d = 0; d < ELEM_PER_THREAD; ++d) { acc[d] *= factor; } m = m_new; // Load V[j] float v_reg[ELEM_PER_THREAD]; { int64_t v_off = ((int64_t)batch * num_kv_heads + kv_head) * seq_len + j; const __half2* v_ptr2 = reinterpret_cast<const __half2*>(v + v_off * HEAD_SIZE); #pragma unroll for (int h2 = 0; h2 < N_HALF2_PER_THREAD; ++h2) { int idx = lane * N_HALF2_PER_THREAD + h2; __half2 val = v_ptr2[idx]; v_reg[h2 * 2 + 0] = __half2float(val.x); v_reg[h2 * 2 + 1] = __half2float(val.y); } } float p = expf(score - m); #pragma unroll for (int d = 0; d < ELEM_PER_THREAD; ++d) { acc[d] += p * v_reg[d]; } } // Normalize and write output l = __shfl_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, l, 0); float inv_l = 1.0f / l; { int64_t o_off = ((int64_t)batch * num_heads + head) * seq_len + q_pos; __half2* out_ptr2 = reinterpret_cast<__half2*>(out + o_off * HEAD_SIZE); #pragma unroll for (int h2 = 0; h2 < N_HALF2_PER_THREAD; ++h2) { int idx = lane * N_HALF2_PER_THREAD + h2; half h0 = __float2half_rn(acc[h2 * 2 + 0] * inv_l); half h1 = __float2half_rn(acc[h2 * 2 + 1] * inv_l); out_ptr2[idx] = __halves2half2(h0, h1); } } } // ... launcher ... ``` This is clean, no shared memory, no divergence issues, and correctly implements causal attention. import torch
import torch.nn as nn
from torch.utils.cpp_extension import load_inline

CUDA_SOURCE = r"""
#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <cuda_fp16.h>
#include <c10/cuda/CUDAStream.h>

#define WARP_SIZE 32

template <int HEAD_SIZE>
__global__ void gqa_causal_nosmem_kernel(
    const at::Half* __restrict__ q,
    const at::Half* __restrict__ k,
    const at::Half* __restrict__ v,
    at::Half* __restrict__ out,
    int num_heads,
    int num_kv_heads,
    int seq_len,
    float scale)
{
    constexpr int ELEM_PER_THREAD = HEAD_SIZE / WARP_SIZE;
    constexpr int N_HALF2_PER_THREAD = ELEM_PER_THREAD / 2;

    int q_pos   = blockIdx.x;
    int head    = blockIdx.y;
    int batch   = blockIdx.z;
    int lane    = threadIdx.x;

    if (q_pos >= seq_len) return;

    int heads_per_kv = num_heads / num_kv_heads;
    int kv_head = head / heads_per_kv;

    // ---- Load Q into registers ----
    float q_reg[ELEM_PER_THREAD];
    {
        int64_t q_off = ((int64_t)batch * num_heads + head) * seq_len + q_pos;
        const __half2* q_ptr2 = reinterpret_cast<const __half2*>(q + q_off * HEAD_SIZE);
        #pragma unroll
        for (int h2 = 0; h2 < N_HALF2_PER_THREAD; ++h2) {
            int idx = lane * N_HALF2_PER_THREAD + h2;
            __half2 val = q_ptr2[idx];
            q_reg[h2 * 2 + 0] = __half2float(val.x);
            q_reg[h2 * 2 + 1] = __half2float(val.y);
        }
    }

    float acc[ELEM_PER_THREAD];
    #pragma unroll
    for (int d = 0; d < ELEM_PER_THREAD; ++d) acc[d] = 0.0f;

    float m = -1e20f;
    float l = 0.0f;

    // ---- Causal attention: attend only to keys 0..q_pos ----
    for (int j = 0; j <= q_pos; ++j) {
        // Load K[j]
        float k_reg[ELEM_PER_THREAD];
        {
            int64_t k_off = ((int64_t)batch * num_kv_heads + kv_head) * seq_len + j;
            const __half2* k_ptr2 = reinterpret_cast<const __half2*>(k + k_off * HEAD_SIZE);
            #pragma unroll
            for (int h2 = 0; h2 < N_HALF2_PER_THREAD; ++h2) {
                int idx = lane * N_HALF2_PER_THREAD + h2;
                __half2 val = k_ptr2[idx];
                k_reg[h2 * 2 + 0] = __half2float(val.x);
                k_reg[h2 * 2 + 1] = __half2float(val.y);
            }
        }

        // Dot product Q·K[j]
        float partial = 0.0f;
        #pragma unroll
        for (int d = 0; d < ELEM_PER_THREAD; ++d) {
            partial += q_reg[d] * k_reg[d];
        }
        #pragma unroll
        for (int offset = WARP_SIZE / 2; offset > 0; offset /= 2) {
            partial += __shfl_down_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, partial, offset);
        }
        float score = __shfl_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, partial, 0) * scale;

        // Online softmax update
        float m_new = fmaxf(m, score);
        float factor = expf(m - m_new);
        if (lane == 0) {
            l = l * factor + expf(score - m_new);
        }
        #pragma unroll
        for (int d = 0; d < ELEM_PER_THREAD; ++d) {
            acc[d] *= factor;
        }
        m = m_new;

        // Load V[j]
        float v_reg[ELEM_PER_THREAD];
        {
            int64_t v_off = ((int64_t)batch * num_kv_heads + kv_head) * seq_len + j;
            const __half2* v_ptr2 = reinterpret_cast<const __half2*>(v + v_off * HEAD_SIZE);
            #pragma unroll
            for (int h2 = 0; h2 < N_HALF2_PER_THREAD; ++h2) {
                int idx = lane * N_HALF2_PER_THREAD + h2;
                __half2 val = v_ptr2[idx];
                v_reg[h2 * 2 + 0] = __half2float(val.x);
                v_reg[h2 * 2 + 1] = __half2float(val.y);
            }
        }

        float p = expf(score - m);
        #pragma unroll
        for (int d = 0; d < ELEM_PER_THREAD; ++d) {
            acc[d] += p * v_reg[d];
        }
    }

    // ---- Normalize and write output ----
    l = __shfl_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, l, 0);
    float inv_l = 1.0f / l;

    {
        int64_t o_off = ((int64_t)batch * num_heads + head) * seq_len + q_pos;
        __half2* out_ptr2 = reinterpret_cast<__half2*>(out + o_off * HEAD_SIZE);
        #pragma unroll
        for (int h2 = 0; h2 < N_HALF2_PER_THREAD; ++h2) {
            int idx = lane * N_HALF2_PER_THREAD + h2;
            half h0 = __float2half_rn(acc[h2 * 2 + 0] * inv_l);
            half h1 = __float2half_rn(acc[h2 * 2 + 1] * inv_l);
            out_ptr2[idx] = __halves2half2(h0, h1);
        }
    }
}

inline void launch_gqa_attn(
    const at::Half* q_ptr, const at::Half* k_ptr, const at::Half* v_ptr,
    at::Half* out_ptr,
    int batch, int num_heads, int num_kv_heads,
    int seq_len, int head_size, float scale)
{
    dim3 grid(seq_len, num_heads, batch);
    dim3 block(WARP_SIZE);
    cudaStream_t stream = at::cuda::getCurrentCUDAStream();

    switch (head_size) {
        case 64:
            gqa_causal_nosmem_kernel<64><<<grid, block, 0, stream>>>(
                q_ptr, k_ptr, v_ptr, out_ptr,
                num_heads, num_kv_heads, seq_len, scale);
            break;
        case 128:
            gqa_causal_nosmem_kernel<128><<<grid, block, 0, stream>>>(
                q_ptr, k_ptr, v_ptr, out_ptr,
                num_heads, num_kv_heads, seq_len, scale);
            break;
        case 256:
            gqa_causal_nosmem_kernel<256><<<grid, block, 0, stream>>>(
                q_ptr, k_ptr, v_ptr, out_ptr,
                num_heads, num_kv_heads, seq_len, scale);
            break;
        default:
            AT_ERROR("gqa_attn: unsupported head_size ", head_size);
    }
}

torch::Tensor gqa_attn_forward(torch::Tensor q, torch::Tensor k, torch::Tensor v,
                               int64_t num_kv_heads, double scale)
{
    TORCH_CHECK(q.is_cuda(), "q must be CUDA");
    TORCH_CHECK(k.is_cuda(), "k must be CUDA");
    TORCH_CHECK(v.is_cuda(), "v must be CUDA");
    TORCH_CHECK(q.scalar_type() == at::kHalf, "q must be fp16");
    TORCH_CHECK(k.scalar_type() == at::kHalf, "k must be fp16");
    TORCH_CHECK(v.scalar_type() == at::kHalf, "v must be fp16");

    int batch = q.size(0);
    int num_heads = q.size(1);
    int seq_len = q.size(2);
    int head_size = q.size(3);

    TORCH_CHECK(k.size(0) == batch && v.size(0) == batch);
    TORCH_CHECK(k.size(1) == num_kv_heads && v.size(1) == num_kv_heads);
    TORCH_CHECK(k.size(2) == seq_len && v.size(2) == seq_len);
    TORCH_CHECK(k.size(3) == head_size && v.size(3) == head_size);

    auto out = torch::empty_like(q);

    launch_gqa_attn(
        q.data_ptr<at::Half>(),
        k.data_ptr<at::Half>(),
        v.data_ptr<at::Half>(),
        out.data_ptr<at::Half>(),
        batch, num_heads, static_cast<int>(num_kv_heads),
        seq_len, head_size, static_cast<float>(scale)
    );
    return out;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &gqa_attn_forward, "GQA causal attention forward");
}
"""

class ModelNew(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, num_heads, num_kv_heads, head_size):
        super().__init__()
        self.num_heads = num_heads
        self.num_kv_heads = num_kv_heads
        self.head_size = head_size
        self.scale = float(head_size) ** -0.5

        self._mod = load_inline(
            name="gqa_causal_nosmem",
            cpp_sources="",
            cuda_sources=CUDA_SOURCE,
            extra_cuda_cflags=["-O3", "--use_fast_math"],
            verbose=False,
        )

    def forward(self, q, k, v):
        return self._mod.forward(q, k, v, self.num_kv_heads, self.scale)
